I haven’t tracked it that neatly, and I wouldn’t want to tell you yes if I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you’ve seen, heard, or felt things that others around you didn’t seem to notice, or when you believed something strongly that others thought wasn’t real or didn’t make sense?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, that’s a harder one to answer without sounding like I’m making it up, but yes, there have been times.\n\nIt happens mostly out by the north pasture. Not every minute of every day, but often enough that I know the place and the time of day matters. Around dusk, when the light gets flat and the irrigation lines are running or settling, I hear voices coming from them. Not like a vague noise that I’m turning into words, either. I mean clear voices, like somebody speaking down inside the lines or through the metal, telling me which rows are poisoned and which ones are blessed.\n\nI know how that sounds. I do. If someone else told me that, I’d probably say, “Well, maybe it’s water pressure, or the wind, or the pipes knocking.” And I’ve tried to explain it that way to myself. But it’s not just clanking. It has words. It tells me things. Sometimes I’ll stand there longer than I should because I’m trying to make out exactly what row it means, and then if someone comes up, they don’t hear it the way I do. They’ll say it’s just the lines or just evening noise.\n\nAnd it’s not only when I’m all wound up or full of energy or anything like that. It still happens on ordinary weeks too, when I’m not especially fired up. That’s part of why I don’t know what to do with it. It feels real when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something very strongly that others around you didn’t share or thought was unreasonable, even when they tried to convince you otherwise?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s… that’s probably the thing my wife and I go around and around about the most.\n\nI know people say I’m reading too much into it, but when I look at the yield maps and the drone reports, there are numbers that don’t belong. Odd numbers in places they shouldn’t be, patterns that don’t line up with what I know about those fields. I’ve worked that land long enough to know what a bad patch looks like, what a low spot does, what drought stress does. This isn’t just me not understanding the technology. It’s like the numbers are placed there as warnings, coded in there, telling me somebody is trying to set things up to take the farm out from under me.\n\nMy wife will sit me down and show me the lender’s paperwork, plain as day, and she’ll say, “Look, this is just the loan statement, this is just the crop report, nobody is doing anything.” And I’ll look at it, and I can see what she’s saying on the surface. I can read the words. But it doesn’t settle it for me, because the warning isn’t in the regular wording. It’s in the figures, the odd numbers, the way they show up in the maps and reports. That’s the part she doesn’t see.\n\nAnd I know that sounds unreasonable to her. She gets frustrated, and I don’t blame her exactly, but I can’t just unknow it once I’ve seen it. It feels like if I ignore it, I’m being careless with everything we’ve built.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt like you didn’t need much sleep, your thoughts were racing, and you were doing things faster or more intensely than usual—like taking on big projects or feeling like you had a special mission?\n\nPatient: Yes… there are times like that, or at least times where I get going so hard it’s like I can’t find the brake.\n\nI’ll be up and moving early, and one thing turns into ten before I’ve even had breakfast. I’ll go from fixing fence to recalibrating the sprayer to calling suppliers, and then by noon I’ve started five more jobs than any crew could reasonably finish in a day. It doesn’t feel strange right at the start, because on a farm there’s always too much to do anyway, so I can tell myself I’m just being productive.\n\nBut it gets… bigger than that. I’ll be moving fast, talking fast, making lists, changing plans, deciding we need to tackle everything at once. I’ll think, “No, this all has to happen now, today, before something gets missed.” And then I’ll look around later and there are tools out, gates half-done, equipment opened up, phone calls made, orders started, and I’ve got more going than one person can keep straight.\n\nSo yes, I’ve had times where I’m doing things faster and more intensely than usual. It can feel important in the moment, like I’m the only one seeing how all the pieces connect and that I’ve got to stay ahead of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least one week where you felt extremely energetic, needed little sleep, and were doing things so fast or intensely that others around you noticed and thought you were out of control or not acting like yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s happened. More than once, if I’m being honest.\n\nDuring those stretches, sleep just… doesn’t seem to matter the way it normally should. Most nights I’ll maybe get two or three hours, and even that’s not proper sleep. It’s more like I sit down in my chair after coming in late, boots still half on sometimes, and I drift off sitting there. Then I’m awake again before the sun, like somebody flipped a switch in me, and I feel charged up, ready to get back outside and work the place like nothing happened.\n\nAnd I know at my age I ought to be dragging after that. A man can’t run a farm on no sleep forever. But in those times, I don’t feel tired the way I should. I’ll look at the clock and think, “Well, that’s enough, there’s work to do,” even if it’s only been a couple hours. Sometimes I don’t sleep at all, just stay up through the night thinking about what needs doing, then when the sky starts getting light I’m already moving.\n\nOther people notice, yes. My wife especially. She’ll say I’m not acting like myself, that I’m going too hard, that nobody needs to be out that early after barely sleeping. But to me, in the middle of it, it feels like I’ve got the energy for it, like stopping would be the strange thing.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you experienced those voices or beliefs when you weren’t feeling especially high, energized, or in the middle of one of those intense periods?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s the part that makes it harder for me to explain away, because it hasn’t only happened when I’m all wound up or running on no sleep.\n\nThere have been quieter weeks where I wasn’t feeling especially big or driven, if that’s what you mean. There have been several weeks where I wasn’t especially energized or grand, and I still believed it. That’s what bothers me, I guess. It doesn’t always wait for those intense periods.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced those voices or unusual beliefs when your mood was completely normal and you weren’t feeling especially high, low, or energized?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I think so. That’s what I was trying to get at before, but maybe I didn’t say it clearly.\n\nThere were several quieter weeks where my mood felt about as normal as it gets for me. I wasn’t especially up, I wasn’t full of big energy, and I wasn’t feeling like I had some grand purpose or that I could handle everything at once. I was just doing the regular farm work, going through the days, tired in the ordinary way a person gets tired.\n\nBut even during those times, I still heard the lines talking. And I still looked at the farm data and felt like it was carrying hidden warnings. I was just doing the regular farm work, going through the days, tired in the ordinary way a person gets tired.\n\nBut even during those times, I still heard the lines talking. And I still looked at the farm data and felt like it was carrying hidden warnings. Not just “maybe there’s something off here,” but like there was something underneath it that I was supposed to notice.\n\nThat’s the part that scares me some, because I can’t always blame it on being wound up or not sleeping. Sometimes everything else seems fairly level, and it’s still there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced those voices or unusual beliefs when you weren't in a high or low mood, and nothing else about your mood was unusual at all?\n\nPatient: Yes. I know I’ve kind of said it already, but yes, there were times when my mood wasn’t doing anything unusual that I could tell.\n\nI mean, I wasn’t up high, wasn’t feeling grand or powerful or like I had some special burst of energy. I wasn’t especially low either. It was more like those quieter weeks where I was just going about things, doing the regular work, trying to keep the farm running, same as any other stretch.\n\nBut even then, the lines would still seem like they were talking. And I’d still look at the farm data and feel like there were warnings hidden in it, like it was carrying a message underneath the numbers. It wasn’t just during those intense periods. That’s the thing that bothers me. Even when everything else in my mood seemed plain and level, that part could still be there."
